<tr><td>↳近 hector</td><td>/ˈhektər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>威吓，欺凌恫吓</td><td>hector the staff<br>恫吓下属</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach hectored his players throughout the second half.  整个下半场教练都在对球员大声恫吓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 awe</td><td>/ɔː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>敬畏</td><td>in awe of<br>对……敬畏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tourists stood in silent awe before the great waterfall.  游客们心怀敬畏，在大瀑布前静静站着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 embolden</td><td>/ɪmˈboʊldən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，使大胆</td><td>embolden the rebels<br>壮了叛乱者的胆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early success emboldened her to ask for a promotion.  初期的成功让她有胆量要求升职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 encourage</td><td>/ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，激励；促进</td><td>encourage students to read<br>鼓励学生阅读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers encourage students to ask difficult questions.  好老师鼓励学生提出有难度的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inspirit</del></td><td>/ɪnˈspɪrɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓舞，激励</td><td>inspirit the weary troops<br>鼓舞疲惫的部队</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her speech inspirited the exhausted volunteers to keep working through the night.  她的演讲鼓舞疲惫的志愿者继续通宵工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cowardice</b></td><td>/ˈkaʊərdɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>怯懦，胆小</td><td>an act of cowardice<br>怯懦之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refusing to speak up for a friend struck him later as cowardice.  没有为朋友说话，事后他觉得那是怯懦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cravenness</del></td><td>/ˈkreɪvənnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>极度怯懦，胆小</td><td>sheer cravenness<br>十足的懦弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His cravenness under fire shocked the men who once admired him.  他在炮火下的懦弱令曾敬佩他的人震惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dastardliness</del></td><td>/ˈdæstərdlinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>卑怯，怯懦无耻</td><td>the dastardliness of the attack<br>这次袭击的卑劣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dastardliness of the ambush disgusted even their own allies.  这次伏击的卑劣连他们自己的盟友都感到恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 gutlessness</del></td><td>/ˈɡʌtləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>没胆量，懦弱</td><td>political gutlessness<br>政治上的懦弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters punished the senator for his gutlessness on the crucial vote.  选民因参议员在关键投票上的懦弱而惩罚了他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 poltroonery</del></td><td>/pɑlˈtrunəri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>怯懦，胆小如鼠</td><td>unforgivable poltroonery<br>不可原谅的怯懦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general denounced such poltroonery before the whole assembled regiment.  将军在全团面前谴责这种怯懦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 pusillanimity</del></td><td>/ˌpjuːsələˈnɪməti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>胆小，懦弱无志气</td><td>diplomatic pusillanimity<br>外交上的软弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their pusillanimity allowed the aggressor to seize the border towns unopposed.  他们的懦弱使侵略者不受阻碍地占领了边境城镇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 spinelessness</del></td><td>/ˈspaɪnləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>没骨气，软弱</td><td>moral spinelessness<br>道德上的软弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His spinelessness in meetings meant unfair decisions were never challenged.  他在会上没骨气，导致不公的决定从未被质疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 courage</td><td>/ˈkɜːrədʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>勇气，胆量</td><td>summon up courage<br>鼓起勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took real courage to admit the mistake in front of everyone.  在众人面前承认错误需要真正的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 guts</td><td>/ɡʌts/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇气，胆量；内脏</td><td>have the guts<br>有胆量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had the guts to quit and start her own company.  她有胆量辞职创办自己的公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pluck</td><td>/plʌk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>敢于面对困难的勇气</td><td>show great pluck<br>表现出极大勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took real pluck to speak up against the whole committee.  敢于当众反对整个委员会需要真正的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 intrepidness</del></td><td>/ɪnˈtrɛpɪdnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇敢，无畏</td><td>the intrepidness of explorers<br>探险者的无畏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The climber's intrepidness carried her to the summit despite the storm.  这位登山者的无畏让她在暴风中登上顶峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nerve</td><td>/nɜːrv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>（坚强的）意志，勇气</td><td>lose one's nerve<br>失去勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lost his nerve at the last moment and never asked the question.  他在最后一刻失去了勇气，始终没有问出那个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 stoutness</del></td><td>/ˈstaʊtnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>刚毅，勇敢坚定</td><td>stoutness of heart<br>心志刚毅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers praised his stoutness during the long, hopeless siege.  村民赞扬他在漫长绝望的围城中的刚毅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 valiance</del></td><td>/ˈvæliəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇猛，英勇</td><td>valiance in battle<br>战场上的英勇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Medals were given for valiance shown during the night attack.  奖章授予在夜袭中表现英勇的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 valor</td><td>/ˈvælər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>英勇，勇猛</td><td>an award for valor<br>英勇奖章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He received a medal for valor after rescuing two trapped miners.  他救出两名被困矿工后获得英勇勋章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 10

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cower</b></td><td>/ˈkaʊər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>畏缩</td><td>cower in the corner<br>在角落里畏缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The frightened puppy cowered under the table during the loud thunderstorm.  雷雨大作时，惊恐的小狗畏缩在桌子下面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fawn</td><td>/fɔːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>阿谀奉承</td><td>fawn on the boss<br>讨好老板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Junior staff fawn on the boss whenever the director visits.  每当董事来访，下级员工就对老板阿谀奉承。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flinch</td><td>/flɪntʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>畏缩，退缩；回避</td><td>flinch from the truth<br>不敢面对真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He never flinched from telling his patients the difficult truth.  他从不回避向病人讲述艰难的真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grovel</td><td>/ˈɡrɑːvl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>卑躬屈膝</td><td>grovel before authority<br>在权威前卑躬屈膝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He refused to grovel before the committee just to keep his post.  他拒绝为了保住职位而在委员会面前卑躬屈膝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quail</td><td>/kweɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>胆怯，畏缩</td><td>quail at the thought<br>一想到就发怵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even veteran soldiers quail at the thought of another winter campaign.  连老兵一想到又一场冬季战役也会畏缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recoil</td><td>/rɪˈkɔɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>退却，畏缩</td><td>recoil in horror<br>惊恐地退缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She recoiled in horror when the lamp revealed the broken door.  灯光照出被砸坏的门时，她惊恐地退缩了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wince</td><td>/wɪns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>畏缩</td><td>wince at the pain<br>因疼痛而龇牙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She winced as the nurse cleaned the deep cut on her arm.  护士清洗她手臂上的深口子时，她疼得直畏缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cozen</b></td><td>/ˈkʌzn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>诱骗</td><td>cozen him into agreeing<br>诱骗他同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler cozened elderly widows out of their modest life savings.  那个骗子诱骗年老的寡妇们交出微薄的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bamboozle</td><td>/bæmˈbuzəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，蒙骗</td><td>bamboozle the customers<br>欺骗顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salesman bamboozled them into buying a worthless extended warranty.  推销员骗他们买了毫无价值的延保。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dupe</td><td>/duːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>dupe trusting investors<br>欺骗轻信的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scheme duped trusting investors out of nearly a million dollars.  这个骗局欺骗轻信的投资者，骗走近百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cheat</td><td>/ˈtʃiːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>欺骗，作弊；骗子</td><td>cheat on an exam<br>考试作弊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cheated the old couple out of their entire life savings.  他骗走了这对老夫妇一生的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deceive</td><td>/dɪˈsiːv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，误导</td><td>deceive the public<br>欺骗公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He deceived his investors with promises of quick and easy profits.  他用轻松快速获利的承诺欺骗了投资者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beguile</td><td>/bɪˈɡaɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗；使着迷</td><td>beguile the audience<br>令观众着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician could beguile an audience for a full hour.  这位魔术师能让观众着迷整整一小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delude</td><td>/dɪˈluːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，使误信</td><td>delude oneself<br>自欺欺人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not delude yourself into thinking the danger has passed.  别欺骗自己，以为危险已经过去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 take in</td><td>/teɪk ˈɪn/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗；收留；领会</td><td>be taken in by<br>被…欺骗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't be taken in by his charming manner and generous promises.  别被他迷人的举止和大方的承诺骗了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cramped</b></td><td>/kræmpt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狭小的，狭窄的</td><td>a cramped office<br>狭小的办公室</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Four of us shared a cramped room above the noisy bakery.  我们四个人挤在嘈杂面包店楼上一间狭小的房间里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confined</td><td>/kənˈfaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狭小的，受限制的</td><td>a confined space<br>狭小的空间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Working in such a confined space made the repair extremely difficult.  在如此狭小的空间作业使维修极为困难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 limited</td><td>/ˈlɪmɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有限的，受限制的</td><td>limited resources<br>有限的资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With limited time we could visit only two of the temples.  时间有限，我们只能参观其中两座寺庙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 restrained</td><td>/rɪˈstreɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>受约束的，克制的</td><td>restrained by rules<br>受规则约束</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His restrained reply hid how angry he actually felt inside.  他克制的回答掩盖了他内心真正的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commodious</td><td>/kəˈmoʊdiəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽敞舒适的</td><td>a commodious apartment<br>宽敞舒适的公寓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They rented a commodious apartment with two sunny balconies.  他们租了一套宽敞舒适、带两个阳台的公寓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spacious</td><td>/ˈspeɪʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽敞的，广阔的</td><td>a spacious kitchen<br>宽敞的厨房</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new office is spacious enough for forty desks.  新办公室很宽敞，能放四十张桌子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>crass</b></td><td>/kræs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗俗的，愚钝的</td><td>crass commercialism<br>赤裸裸的商业气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His crass joke silenced the entire table at dinner.  他粗俗的玩笑让整桌人都沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coarse</td><td>/kɔːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，表面不平整的；粗俗的</td><td>coarse language<br>粗俗的言语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers used coarse language that shocked the visiting students.  工人们说的粗话让来访的学生们大吃一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crude</td><td>/ˈkruːd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，未加工的；粗俗的</td><td>crude oil<br>原油</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The furniture was crude but surprisingly sturdy and very cheap.  这家具做得粗糙，却意外结实又便宜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rude</td><td>/ˈruːd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁的，无礼的</td><td>a rude remark<br>无礼的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was rude of him to leave without thanking the host.  他不谢主人就离开，很没礼貌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 incult</del></td><td>/ɪnˈkʌlt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗野的，未开化的</td><td>incult manners<br>粗野的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The travelers found the mountain villages poor and their manners incult.  旅行者发现山村贫穷，村民举止粗野。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncouth</td><td>/ʌnˈkuːθ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁的，无教养的</td><td>uncouth table manners<br>粗野的餐桌举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His uncouth remarks at dinner offended nearly everyone at the table.  他在晚餐上粗鲁的言辞几乎惹恼了同桌所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vulgar</td><td>/ˈvʌlɡər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗俗的，无教养的</td><td>vulgar display of wealth<br>粗俗的炫富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their vulgar display of wealth embarrassed even their closest friends.  他们粗俗的炫富让连最亲近的朋友都感到难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 civilized</td><td>/ˈsɪvəˌlaɪzd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>文明的，有教养的</td><td>civilized behavior<br>文明的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They settled the dispute in a calm, civilized manner over tea.  他们喝茶时以平静文明的方式解决了争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 polished</td><td>/ˈpɑːlɪʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的</td><td>polished manners<br>优雅得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His polished manners impressed everyone at the formal dinner.  他优雅得体的举止让正式晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 refined</td><td>/rəˈfaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精炼的，纯净的；有教养的</td><td>refined sugar<br>精制糖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refined sugar has been stripped of every impurity.  精制糖已去除了所有杂质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 urbane</td><td>/ərˈbeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>彬彬有礼的，文雅的</td><td>an urbane host<br>文雅的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our urbane host guided the conversation with easy wit and grace.  我们文雅的主人以轻松的机智和风度引导着谈话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 genteel</td><td>/dʒenˈtiːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，不粗俗的</td><td>genteel manners<br>有教养的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her genteel accent betrayed an expensive and very traditional upbringing.  她文雅的口音透露出昂贵而传统的成长背景。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>crave</b></td><td>/kreɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>热望</td><td>crave attention<br>渴望关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After weeks in the desert, they craved nothing but cold clean water.  在沙漠里待了几周后，他们只渴望干净的凉水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 need</td><td>/ˈniːd/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>需要，必需</td><td>need urgent help<br>需要紧急帮助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Plants need sunlight and water to grow properly indoors.  室内植物需要阳光和水才能正常生长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 yearn</td><td>/ˈjɜːrn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>渴望，向往</td><td>yearn for freedom<br>渴望自由</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After years abroad he yearned for the food of his childhood.  在国外多年后，他渴望儿时的食物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 desire</td><td>/dɪˈzaɪər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>渴望，想要；欲望</td><td>desire for success<br>对成功的渴望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She desired nothing more than a quiet evening at home.  她只想在家过一个安静的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pine</td><td>/paɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>渴望，思念</td><td>pine for home<br>思念家乡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After a year abroad he began to pine for the food of home.  在国外一年后，他开始渴望家乡的食物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thirst</td><td>/ˈθɜːrst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>渴望；口渴</td><td>a thirst for knowledge<br>求知欲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He thirsted for recognition far more than for money.  他对认可的渴望远超对金钱的渴望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 itch</td><td>/ˈɪtʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>渴望；发痒</td><td>itch to travel<br>渴望去旅行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children were itching to open their presents early.  孩子们急着想早点拆礼物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 long</td><td>/ˈlɔːŋ/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>不及物动词、形容词</td><td>渴望；长的</td><td>long for company<br>渴望有人陪伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After months at sea he longed for his family's cooking.  在海上数月后，他渴望家人做的饭菜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 yen</td><td>/ˈjen/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>强烈的渴望</td><td>a yen for adventure<br>对冒险的渴望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a sudden yen for spicy noodles at midnight.  她半夜突然特别想吃辣面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spurn</td><td>/spɜːrn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>摈弃，拒绝</td><td>spurn an offer<br>断然拒绝提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spurned the generous offer without giving any explanation at all.  她断然拒绝了这个优厚的提议，且不作任何解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>craven</b></td><td>/ˈkreɪvn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常懦弱的，因胆小而遭人鄙视的</td><td>a craven retreat<br>怯懦的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His craven silence during the trial disappointed everyone who had trusted him.  他在庭审中懦弱的沉默让所有信任过他的人失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cowardly</td><td>/ˈkaʊərdli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>怯懦的，胆小的</td><td>a cowardly attack<br>卑怯的袭击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was cowardly to blame the mistake on his assistant.  把错误推给助手是怯懦的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gutless</td><td>/ˈɡʌtləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没胆量的，懦弱的</td><td>a gutless decision<br>懦弱的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team looked gutless in the second half of the match.  球队下半场表现得毫无斗志。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pusillanimous</td><td>/ˌpjuːsɪˈlænɪməs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小的，懦弱的</td><td>a pusillanimous response<br>软弱的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the government's pusillanimous silence a betrayal of victims.  批评者称政府懦弱的沉默是对受害者的背叛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spineless</td><td>/ˈspaɪnləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没骨气的，软弱的</td><td>a spineless coward<br>没骨气的胆小鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was too spineless to stand up to his own manager.  他太没骨气，不敢反抗自己的经理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dauntless</td><td>/ˈdɔntləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无畏的，勇敢的</td><td>dauntless courage<br>无畏的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dauntless nurse worked through shelling to reach the wounded.  这位无畏的护士在炮击中赶去救伤员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fearless</td><td>/ˈfɪrləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无畏的，大胆的</td><td>a fearless reporter<br>无畏的记者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was fearless in questioning the most powerful men present.  她敢于质问在场最有权势的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gallant</td><td>/ˈɡælənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>英勇的</td><td>a gallant defense<br>英勇的防守</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallant paratroopers jumped from the plane without a moment's hesitation.  英勇的伞兵们毫不犹豫地跳出了飞机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gutsy</td><td>/ˈɡʌtsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有勇气的，大胆的</td><td>a gutsy performance<br>有勇气的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a gutsy move to bet everything on one project.  把一切押在一个项目上是很有勇气的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intrepid</td><td>/ɪnˈtrepɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的</td><td>an intrepid explorer<br>无畏的探险家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The intrepid explorer crossed the frozen strait alone in the middle of winter.  这位大胆的探险家在隆冬时节独自穿越了结冰的海峡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stalwart</td><td>/ˈstɔːlwərt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定忠诚的；强壮的</td><td>a stalwart supporter<br>坚定的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remained a stalwart supporter of the project through every setback.  历经挫折，她始终是这个项目的坚定支持者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stout</td><td>/ˈstaʊt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的，勇敢的；粗壮的</td><td>stout resistance<br>顽强的抵抗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The townspeople put up a stout defense against the raiders.  镇民对袭击者进行了顽强的抵抗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 stouthearted</del></td><td>/ˌstaʊtˈhɑːrtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇敢的</td><td>stouthearted defenders<br>勇敢的守卫者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A handful of stouthearted defenders held the bridge until dawn.  少数勇敢的守卫者守住桥梁直到黎明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 valiant</td><td>/ˈvæliənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇敢的，英勇的</td><td>a valiant effort<br>英勇的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A valiant firefighter carried two children out of the burning building.  一位英勇的消防员把两个孩子抱出了燃烧的大楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 valorous</td><td>/ˈvælərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇猛的，英勇的</td><td>a valorous knight<br>英勇的骑士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Songs still honor the valorous soldiers who held the bridge.  歌曲仍在颂扬守住那座桥的英勇士兵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>contingency</b></td><td>/kənˈtɪndʒənsi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>可能发生的事</td><td>a contingency plan<br>应急计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good manager prepares for every contingency, however unlikely it may seem.  好的管理者会为每一种可能发生的事做准备，无论看来多不可能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 case</td><td>/ˈkeɪs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>情况，案例；箱子</td><td>in that case<br>那样的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In every case we studied, the same pattern appeared again.  在我们研究的每个案例中，同样的模式都再次出现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contingent</td><td>/kənˈtɪndʒənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>视条件而定的；分遣队</td><td>contingent on approval<br>取决于批准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bonus is contingent on the company meeting its yearly targets.  奖金取决于公司是否达成年度目标。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eventuality</td><td>/ɪˌventʃuˈæləti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>可能发生的事</td><td>prepare for every eventuality<br>为各种可能做准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good managers plan for every eventuality, however unlikely it seems.  优秀的管理者会为各种可能做好准备，无论看来多不可能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 possibility</td><td>/ˌpɑːsəˈbɪləti/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>可能性，可能的事</td><td>rule out the possibility<br>排除这种可能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We cannot ignore the possibility of another storm this week.  我们不能忽视本周再来一场风暴的可能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>credence</b></td><td>/ˈkriːdns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>相信，信任</td><td>lend credence to<br>使…显得可信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Recent excavations lend credence to the old story of a buried city.  近期的挖掘使那个关于地下古城的传说变得可信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belief</td><td>/bɪˈliːf/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>信念，相信</td><td>a firm belief<br>坚定的信念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her belief in the project never wavered, even after the setbacks.  即使遭遇挫折，她对项目的信念也从未动摇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 credit</td><td>/ˈkredɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>表扬，赞扬；信任</td><td>take credit for<br>把功劳归于自己</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She deserves credit for keeping the project alive.  她值得称赞，因为她让这个项目坚持下来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 faith</td><td>/ˈfeɪθ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>信任，信念；信仰</td><td>have faith in<br>信任，相信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They kept faith in their coach through a terrible season.  在糟糕的赛季里他们始终信任教练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trust</td><td>/trʌst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>信任，信赖</td><td>win someone's trust<br>赢得某人信任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It takes years to build trust and one lie to lose it.  建立信任要数年，一句谎言就足以失去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reliance</td><td>/rɪˈlaɪəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>依赖，信赖</td><td>heavy reliance on<br>严重依赖…</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village's reliance on a single crop made it very vulnerable.  村子对单一作物的依赖使其非常脆弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 doubt</td><td>/ˈdaʊt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>怀疑，疑虑</td><td>cast doubt on<br>使人怀疑…</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is little doubt that the data was carelessly recorded.  几乎可以肯定，这些数据记录得很草率。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 skepticism</td><td>/ˈskeptɪˌsɪzəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>怀疑，怀疑态度</td><td>widespread public skepticism<br>普遍的公众怀疑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scientists greeted the claim with polite but obvious skepticism.  科学家对该说法表现出礼貌却明显的怀疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>credulous</b></td><td>/ˈkredʒələs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻信的，易受骗的</td><td>a credulous audience<br>轻信的听众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The advertisement was designed to fool credulous shoppers with fake reviews.  这则广告用虚假评论来欺骗轻信的购物者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 believing</td><td>/bɪˈliːvɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相信的，信任的</td><td>a believing heart<br>一颗信任的心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The believing crowd accepted every word of the sermon without question.  轻信的人群不加质疑地接受了布道中的每一句话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unwary</td><td>/ʌnˈweri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不警惕的，粗心的</td><td>trap the unwary traveler<br>诱骗粗心的旅客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such websites are designed to trick the unwary buyer.  这类网站专门欺骗不加防范的买家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gullible</td><td>/ˈɡʌləbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易受骗的，轻信的</td><td>gullible investors<br>易受骗的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scammers target gullible people with promises of quick, easy riches.  骗子用快速致富的承诺瞄准易受骗的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 naive</td><td>/naɪˈiːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天真的，幼稚的</td><td>naive assumptions<br>天真的假设</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was naive to expect honesty from someone with that record.  指望有那种记录的人诚实太天真了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsuspecting</td><td>/ˌʌnsəˈspektɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>未察觉的，不怀疑的</td><td>an unsuspecting victim<br>毫无察觉的受害者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief followed an unsuspecting tourist down the crowded alley.  小偷跟着一位毫无察觉的游客走进拥挤的小巷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disbelieving</td><td>/dɪsbəˈliːvɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相信的，怀疑的</td><td>a disbelieving stare<br>不相信的凝视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave him a disbelieving look and asked for proof.  她怀疑地看了他一眼，要他给出证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 skeptical</td><td>/ˈskeptəkəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>怀疑的，不轻信的</td><td>skeptical of claims<br>对说法持怀疑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters remain skeptical about promises made just before an election.  选民对选前才作出的承诺仍持怀疑态度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>caterwaul</b></del></td><td>/ˈkætərwɔːl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>嚎叫</td><td>caterwaul all night<br>整夜嚎叫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two stray cats caterwauled outside my window most of the night.  两只流浪猫在我窗外嚎叫了大半夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 yowl</td><td>/jaʊl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>嚎叫，哀号</td><td>yowl in pain<br>痛苦地嚎叫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A stray cat yowled outside our window until dawn.  一只流浪猫在我们窗外嚎叫到天亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shriek</td><td>/ˈʃriːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>尖叫，尖声喊</td><td>shriek with laughter<br>尖声大笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children shrieked as the roller coaster plunged downward.  过山车俯冲时孩子们尖叫起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 howl</td><td>/ˈhaʊl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>长嚎，怒吼</td><td>howl at the moon<br>对月长嚎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wolves howled somewhere beyond the frozen ridge that night.  那晚狼在冰封山脊外某处长嚎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scream</td><td>/ˈskriːm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>尖叫，喊叫</td><td>scream for help<br>尖声呼救</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She screamed for help when the water reached her knees.  水淹到膝盖时她尖声呼救。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shrill</td><td>/ˈʃrɪl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>尖利的，刺耳的</td><td>a shrill whistle<br>刺耳的哨声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A shrill alarm woke everyone in the building at midnight.  刺耳的警报半夜惊醒了楼里所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 yelp</td><td>/ˈjelp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>短促尖叫，吠叫</td><td>yelp in surprise<br>惊叫一声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puppy yelped when someone stepped on its tail.  有人踩到尾巴时小狗尖叫了一声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
